<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give an exact uniform sampler for nonnegative integer contingency tables with arbitrary prescribed margins. It terminates almost surely and has expected bit complexity polynomial in both dimensions and the binary length of the margins. No positivity, balance, sparsity, or fixed-dimension assumption is required.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For arbitrary nonnegative integer margins of equal total, the following algorithms exist in the model of unbiased random bits and bit operations.* 1. *Given an integer $k\ge1$, a bounded-time algorithm outputs a table in $\Omega(r,c)$ whose law is within $2^{-k}$ in total variation of the uniform law. Its running time is polynomial in $m,n,\log(N+1),k$.* 2. *An algorithm outputs an exactly uniform table in $\Omega(r,c)$, terminates almost surely, and has expected running time polynomial in $m,n,\log(N+1)$.* *The polynomials are uniform over all margin vectors. No positivity, sparsity, balance, or fixed-dimension hypothesis is imposed.
  - Explanation: Provides bounded almost-uniform sampling and exact sampling with polynomial expected bit cost for arbitrary margins.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-repair)): The repair is a feasible transversal. For each fixed ordered pair $(s,t)$ it is injective on small states and extends to an injection $$(X,A)\longmapsto \begin{cases} (\mathcal R(X),A),&v\in\mathcal S,\\ (\mathcal R(X),A+e_v),&v\in\mathcal B \end{cases} \quad(A\in\mathcal F_X).$$ Thus $f(X)\le f(\mathcal R(X))$. Order small slots by row and then by column. If $t$ follows $s$, the repair leaves every slot preceding $s$ unchanged. When additionally $x_s=l-1,q_s=l$, its repaired value at $s$ is $l$ if $i'=i$ and $l-1$ otherwise.
  - Explanation: Repairs a defect injectively into a feasible transversal and preserves the stated ordered prefixes.
- **C3** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-small-entry)): Consider the uniform law on all nonnegative integer tables with prescribed margins, with at most $m$ rows and $n$ columns and no entry restrictions. If both margins incident to a cell are at least $a\ge4d$, then, for every integer $t$ with $0<t\le a/2$, $$\mathbb{P}\{\text{the cell's entry is less than }t\}\le\frac{4td^3}{a}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the probability of a small cell entry when its incident margins are sufficiently large.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-stationary-success)): The masses satisfy $$\label{eq:stationary-mass-bounds} 0<Z_0\le\Lambda\le(1+d^2)Z_0, \qquad \pi_{\min}\ge(C+1)^{-2d}.$$ Draw $X$ with law $\pi$ and then a uniform $A\in\mathcal F_X$. Declare success if $X$ is a transversal and every large entry of $A$ is at least $L$. Subtracting $L$ from these entries gives a uniform original table conditional on success, and $$\label{eq:unpadding-success} \mathbb{P}\{\text{success}\}\ge\frac{1}{2(1+d^2)}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds stationary mass and gives conditional uniformity after successful unpadding.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-laminar-signature)): With no balanced slots summed, every two-removal matrix just defined has at most one positive eigenvalue.
  - Explanation: Controls the positive eigenvalues of the unsummed two-removal matrices.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pair-summation)): Fix a box-valid display $p$ on the old active coordinates, a fresh slot of capacity $B\ge2$, and fixed context and balanced sums as above. Choose its displayed occupancy $D'\in\{B,B+1\}$ so that $$\label{eq:pair-total} c+\|p\|+D'+\sum_{s\in\mathcal R}B_s=T+2.$$ Write $\Phi(r,a,b)$ for the weight after the balanced sums, with old values $r$ and fresh pair $(a,b)$. The box-valid displays $(p,j,D'-j)$ are indexed by $$\mathcal J= \begin{cases} \{0,\ldots,D'\},&D'=B,\\ \{1,\ldots,D'-1\},&D'=B+1. \end{cases}$$ Their two-removal matrices have the blocks $$\begin{aligned} (V_j)_{ih}&=\Phi(p-e_i-e_h,j,D'-j),\\ S(l)_i&=\Phi(p-e_i,l,D'-1-l),\\ u_j&=\Phi(p,j-1,D'-1-j). \end{aligned}$$ Thus $V_j$ records two old removals, $S(l)$ one old and one fresh removal, and $u_j$ one removal from each fresh coordinate of display $j$. Put $S(l)=0$ outside $0\le l\le D'-1$ and $u_j=0$ outside $1\le j\le D'-1$. Suppose each matrix $$\label{eq:pair-block} M_j=\begin{pmatrix} V_j&S(j-1)&S(j)\\ S(j-1)^{\mathsf T}&u_{j-1}&u_j\\ S(j)^{\mathsf T}&u_j&u_{j+1} \end{pmatrix},\qquad j\in\mathcal J,$$ has at most one positive eigenvalue. The entries $u_{j-1}$ and $u_{j+1}$ are the double removals at the first and second fresh coordinates, respectively. For every real old vector $h$ satisfying $\sum_{l=0}^{D'-1}h^{\mathsf T}S(l)=0$, define $$\label{eq:pair-cumulative} g_j=\frac{\sum_{l=0}^{j-1}h^{\mathsf T}S(l)}{u_j} \quad(1\le j\le D'-1),\qquad g_j=0\quad\text{otherwise}.$$ All these divisions are valid, and $$\label{eq:pair-one} -h^{\mathsf T}V_jh\ \ge\ 2u_jg_j^2-u_{j-1}g_{j-1}^2-u_{j+1}g_{j+1}^2, \qquad j\in\mathcal J.$$ If $D'=B$, the matrix $\sum_{j=0}^B V_j$ has at most one positive eigenvalue. If $D'=B+1$, then $$\label{eq:pair-weighted} 2\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}u_jg_j^2 \ \le\ -h^{\mathsf T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}j(D'-j)V_j\right)h.$$
  - Explanation: Preserves the signature under balanced summation and bounds the weighted quadratic evaluation under the balance condition.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-signature)): Fix $0<\eta<1$. For arbitrary box-valid fixed context $\sigma$, arbitrary balanced-slot summation set $\mathcal R$, and arbitrary box-valid active display $p$ satisfying <a href="#eq:two-removal-total" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-removal-total">[eq:two-removal-total]</a>, the matrix $$\bigl(\Phi_{\sigma,\mathcal R}(p-e_i-e_h)\bigr)_{i,h}$$ has at most one positive eigenvalue.
  - Explanation: Extends the signature bound to arbitrary box-valid contexts and balanced-slot sums.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-child-logconcavity)): At every context with positive transversal mass, the child masses $(z_j)_{j=0}^U$ are log-concave and their positive support is an integer interval. If $z_a,z_b>0$ and $a\le j\le b$, then $z_j\ge\min(z_a,z_b)$.
  - Explanation: Gives log-concavity and interval support for positive conditional child masses.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transport)): Let $\sigma$ be a row-major context, and let children $l-1,l$ of its next slot have positive masses $z_-,z_+$ and means $H_-,H_+$. Then $$\label{eq:conditional-transport} \min(z_-,z_+)(H_--H_+)^2 \le A_0 E_\sigma(H),\qquad A_0=2+2d(U+1)^2.$$
  - Explanation: Controls adjacent conditional means by unit-exchange energy.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-poincare)): For every real function $H$ on the feasible small graph, $$\label{eq:graph-poincare} \Lambda\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_{f/\Lambda}H \le \left((1+2d^2)4d^2(U+1)^6+2\right)E(H) \le 770d^4U^6 E(H).$$ In particular, the feasible graph is connected.
  - Explanation: Bounds graph variance by energy and proves connectivity.
- **C11** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-dense-draw)): Let a rectangular block have at most $m$ rows and $n$ columns, nonnegative integer margins of equal total less than $C$, and every margin at least $L=d^{12}$. For each integer $h\ge1$, there is an algorithm that always outputs a feasible integer table and whose law is within $2^{-h}$ in total variation of the uniform law on the block’s feasible tables. Its worst-case bit running time is polynomial in $d,b,h$. Empty blocks, and blocks having a single row or a single column, have deterministic completion algorithms with the same guarantee.
  - Explanation: Provides a bounded almost-uniform draw from dense completion blocks, including deterministic degenerate blocks.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bin-acceptance)): An ideal trial succeeds with probability at least $1/64$. Conditional on success, its output is exactly uniform on the feasible integer tables.
  - Explanation: Gives a constant acceptance lower bound and exact uniformity conditional on acceptance for an ideal bin trial.
- **C13** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-integral-interpolation)): Let $S_1,S_2,S_0$ be bounded finite unions of open axis-aligned boxes in $\mathbb{R}^q$, and let $0<t<1$, with $(1-t)S_1+tS_2\subset S_0$. Let $f_1,f_2,f_0$ be positive continuous functions on a compact box containing the closures of these sets. If $$f_0((1-t)x+ty)\ge f_1(x)^{1-t}f_2(y)^t \quad(x\in S_1,\ y\in S_2),$$ then $$\label{eq:integral-interpolation} \int_{S_0}f_0\ge \left(\int_{S_1}f_1\right)^{1-t} \left(\int_{S_2}f_2\right)^t.$$ In particular, for finite unions $S,D_0$ of open boxes in the sampling cube, $$\label{eq:dense-logconcave-interpolation} \int_{(1-t)S+tD_0}\rho\ge \left(\int_S\rho\right)^{1-t} \left(\int_{D_0}\rho\right)^t.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the interpolated integral for finite unions of open boxes with positive continuous weights.
- **C14** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-mixing)): Let $P$ be a reversible kernel on a nonempty finite set $V$, with stationary probabilities $\pi(x)>0$ and $P(x,x)\ge1/2$. Set $Q(x,y)=\pi(x)P(x,y)$ and $$\mathcal E_P(H)=\sum_{\{x,y\}\subseteq V} Q(x,y)(H(x)-H(y))^2,$$ where unordered pairs have distinct endpoints. If $\mathop{\mathrm{Var}}_\pi H\le K\mathcal E_P(H)$ for all $H$, with $K\ge1$, then, writing $\pi_{\min}=\min_{x\in V}\pi(x)$, for every integer $t\ge0$, $$\label{eq:elementary-mixing} \lVert P^t(x,\cdot)-\pi\rVert_{\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}} \le \pi_{\min}^{-1}e^{-t/K}.$$ If, for some $\kappa>0$, every cut satisfies $$Q(A,A^c):=\sum_{x\in A,\,y\notin A}Q(x,y) \ge\kappa\min\{\pi(A),\pi(A^c)\},$$ then the variance inequality holds with $K=2/\kappa^2$.
  - Explanation: Turns variance or conductance bounds into a mixing estimate for lazy reversible finite kernels.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bin-gap)): For every nontrivial cut of the bin states, its stationary edge conductance divided by the smaller stationary side mass is at least $$\kappa_B=\frac1{10^5d^2K_0}.$$ Starting at any bin, after $H=d^{50}(h+1)^2$ steps the total-variation distance from $\pi_B$ is at most $2^{-4(h+d)}$.
  - Explanation: Bounds bin-chain conductance and mixing from any initial bin.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-small-chain)): The kernel $P$ is lazy and reversible for $\pi(X)=f(X)/\Lambda$. On every edge it satisfies $$P(X,Y)\ge \frac{1}{128d^2} \min\left\{1,\frac{f(Y)}{f(X)}\right\}.$$ If there is more than one state, its inverse spectral gap is at most $d^{160}$. Moreover $\pi_{\min}\ge(C+1)^{-2d}$, and, for every $t\ge0$, $$\label{eq:small-mixing} \mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(P^t(X_0,\cdot),\pi\bigr) \le (C+1)^{2d}\exp(-t/d^{160}).$$ A one-state chain is already stationary.
  - Explanation: Bounds edge transitions and mixing of the ideal completion-weighted small chain.
- **C17** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-almost-uniform)): The preceding procedure returns a feasible table with law $p_k$ satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(p_k,\operatorname{Uniform}(\Omega)\bigr)\le2^{-k}.$$ Its worst-case number of bit operations is bounded by a fixed polynomial in $d,b,k$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the output error of the implemented finite procedure and its worst-case bit work.
- **C18** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-prop-law-tabulation)): For fixed margins and $k$, the entire law $p_k$ can be computed exactly in $$2^{500db}\operatorname{poly}(d,b,k)$$ bit operations. It has a common dyadic denominator: $p_k(z)=a_z/2^R$, where $a_z$ are nonnegative integers and $R$ is bounded by a fixed polynomial in $d,b,k$. The sizes of all enumerated state spaces are independent of $k$.
  - Explanation: Tabulates the actual bounded sampler law exactly with a common dyadic denominator and accuracy-independent state spaces.
- **C19** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-exact-sampling)): There is an algorithm using unbiased random bits that terminates almost surely, outputs an exactly uniform element of $\Omega$, and has expected bit running time polynomial in $d,b$.
  - Explanation: Provides exactly uniform output with almost-sure termination and polynomial expected bit cost.

Its running time is polynomial in $m,n,\\log(N+1),k$.*\n\n2.  *An algorithm outputs an exactly uniform table in $\\Omega(r,c)$, terminates almost surely, and has expected running time polynomial in $m,n,\\log(N+1)$.*\n\n*The polynomials are uniform over all margin vectors. No positivity, sparsity, balance, or fixed-dimension hypothesis is imposed.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-115.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For arbitrary nonnegative integer margins of equal total, the following algorithms exist in the model of unbiased random bits and bit operations.*\n\n1.  *Given an integer $k\\ge1$, a bounded-time algorithm outputs a table in $\\Omega(r,c)$ whose law is within $2^{-k}$ in total variation of the uniform law. Its running time is polynomial in $m,n,\\log(N+1),k$.*\n\n2.  *An algorithm outputs an exactly uniform table in $\\Omega(r,c)$, terminates almost surely, and has expected running time polynomial in $m,n,\\log(N+1)$.*\n\n*The polynomials are uniform over all margin vectors.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-repair",
        "exact_quote": "The repair is a feasible transversal. For each fixed ordered pair $(s,t)$ it is injective on small states and extends to an injection $$(X,A)\\longmapsto\n \\begin{cases}\n (\\mathcal R(X),A),&v\\in\\mathcal S,\\\\\n (\\mathcal R(X),A+e_v),&v\\in\\mathcal B\n \\end{cases}\n \\quad(A\\in\\mathcal F_X).$$ Thus $f(X)\\le f(\\mathcal R(X))$. Order small slots by row and then by column. If $t$ follows $s$, the repair leaves every slot preceding $s$ unchanged. When additionally $x_s=l-1,q_s=l$, its repaired value at $s$ is $l$ if $i'=i$ and $l-1$ otherwise.",
        "source_id": "PM-115.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-entry",
        "exact_quote": "Consider the uniform law on all nonnegative integer tables with prescribed margins, with at most $m$ rows and $n$ columns and no entry restrictions.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pair-summation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a box-valid display $p$ on the old active coordinates, a fresh slot of capacity $B\\ge2$, and fixed context and balanced sums as above. Choose its displayed occupancy $D'\\in\\{B,B+1\\}$ so that $$\\label{eq:pair-total}\n c+|p|+D'+\\sum_{s\\in\\mathcal R}B_s=T+2.$$ Write $\\Phi(r,a,b)$ for the weight after the balanced sums, with old values $r$ and fresh pair $(a,b)$. The box-valid displays $(p,j,D'-j)$ are indexed by $$\\mathcal J=\n \\begin{cases}\n \\{0,\\ldots,D'\\},&D'=B,\\\\\n \\{1,\\ldots,D'-1\\},&D'=B+1.\n \\end{cases}$$ Their two-removal matrices have the blocks $$\\begin{aligned}\n (V_j)_{ih}&=\\Phi(p-e_i-e_h,j,D'-j),\\\\\n S(l)_i&=\\Phi(p-e_i,l,D'-1-l),\\\\\n u_j&=\\Phi(p,j-1,D'-1-j).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Thus $V_j$ records two old removals, $S(l)$ one old and one fresh removal, and $u_j$ one removal from each fresh coordinate of display $j$. Put $S(l)=0$ outside $0\\le l\\le D'-1$ and $u_j=0$ outside $1\\le j\\le D'-1$. Suppose each matrix $$\\label{eq:pair-block}\n M_j=\\begin{pmatrix}\n V_j&S(j-1)&S(j)\\\\\n S(j-1)^{\\mathsf T}&u_{j-1}&u_j\\\\\n S(j)^{\\mathsf T}&u_j&u_{j+1}\n \\end{pmatrix},\\qquad j\\in\\mathcal J,$$ has at most one positive eigenvalue. The entries $u_{j-1}$ and $u_{j+1}$ are the double removals at the first and second fresh coordinates, respectively. For every real old vector $h$ satisfying $\\sum_{l=0}^{D'-1}h^{\\mathsf T}S(l)=0$, define $$\\label{eq:pair-cumulative}\n g_j=\\frac{\\sum_{l=0}^{j-1}h^{\\mathsf T}S(l)}{u_j}\n       \\quad(1\\le j\\le D'-1),\\qquad\n g_j=0\\quad\\text{otherwise}.$$ All these divisions are valid, and $$\\label{eq:pair-one}\n -h^{\\mathsf T}V_jh\\ \\ge\\\n 2u_jg_j^2-u_{j-1}g_{j-1}^2-u_{j+1}g_{j+1}^2,\n \\qquad j\\in\\mathcal J.$$ If $D'=B$, the matrix $\\sum_{j=0}^B V_j$ has at most one positive eigenvalue. If $D'=B+1$, then $$\\label{eq:pair-weighted}\n 2\\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}u_jg_j^2\n \\ \\le\\\n -h^{\\mathsf T}\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}j(D'-j)V_j\\right)h.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-115.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pair-summation",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a box-valid display $p$ on the old active coordinates, a fresh slot of capacity $B\\ge2$, and fixed context and balanced sums as above. The box-valid displays $(p,j,D'-j)$ are indexed by $$\\mathcal J=\n \\begin{cases}\n \\{0,\\ldots,D'\\},&D'=B,\\\\\n \\{1,\\ldots,D'-1\\},&D'=B+1.\n \\end{cases}$$ Their two-removal matrices have the blocks $$\\begin{aligned}\n (V_j)_{ih}&=\\Phi(p-e_i-e_h,j,D'-j),\\\\\n S(l)_i&=\\Phi(p-e_i,l,D'-1-l),\\\\\n u_j&=\\Phi(p,j-1,D'-1-j).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Thus $V_j$ records two old removals, $S(l)$ one old and one fresh removal, and $u_j$ one removal from each fresh coordinate of display $j$. Put $S(l)=0$ outside $0\\le l\\le D'-1$ and $u_j=0$ outside $1\\le j\\le D'-1$. Suppose each matrix $$\\label{eq:pair-block}\n M_j=\\begin{pmatrix}\n V_j&S(j-1)&S(j)\\\\\n S(j-1)^{\\mathsf T}&u_{j-1}&u_j\\\\\n S(j)^{\\mathsf T}&u_j&u_{j+1}\n \\end{pmatrix},\\qquad j\\in\\mathcal J,$$ has at most one positive eigenvalue. The entries $u_{j-1}$ and $u_{j+1}$ are the double removals at the first and second fresh coordinates, respectively. For every real old vector $h$ satisfying $\\sum_{l=0}^{D'-1}h^{\\mathsf T}S(l)=0$, define $$\\label{eq:pair-cumulative}\n g_j=\\frac{\\sum_{l=0}^{j-1}h^{\\mathsf T}S(l)}{u_j}\n       \\quad(1\\le j\\le D'-1),\\qquad\n g_j=0\\quad\\text{otherwise}.$$ All these divisions are valid, and $$\\label{eq:pair-one}\n -h^{\\mathsf T}V_jh\\ \\ge\\\n 2u_jg_j^2-u_{j-1}g_{j-1}^2-u_{j+1}g_{j+1}^2,\n \\qquad j\\in\\mathcal J.$$ If $D'=B$, the matrix $\\sum_{j=0}^B V_j$ has at most one positive eigenvalue. If $D'=B+1$, then $$\\label{eq:pair-weighted}\n 2\\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}u_jg_j^2\n \\ \\le\\\n -h^{\\mathsf T}\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}j(D'-j)V_j\\right)h.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-115.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix a box-valid display $p$ on the old active coordinates, a fresh slot of capacity $B\\ge2$, and fixed context and balanced sums as above.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transport",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\sigma$ be a row-major context, and let children $l-1,l$ of its next slot have positive masses $z_-,z_+$ and means $H_-,H_+$. Then $$\\label{eq:conditional-transport}\n \\min(z_-,z_+)(H_--H_+)^2\n \\le A_0 E_\\sigma(H),\\qquad A_0=2+2d(U+1)^2.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-115.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transport",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-dense-draw",
        "exact_quote": "Let a rectangular block have at most $m$ rows and $n$ columns, nonnegative integer margins of equal total less than $C$, and every margin at least $L=d^{12}$. For each integer $h\\ge1$, there is an algorithm that always outputs a feasible integer table and whose law is within $2^{-h}$ in total variation of the uniform law on the block’s feasible tables. Its worst-case bit running time is polynomial in $d,b,h$. Empty blocks, and blocks having a single row or a single column, have deterministic completion algorithms with the same guarantee.",
        "source_id": "PM-115.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-integral-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S_1,S_2,S_0$ be bounded finite unions of open axis-aligned boxes in $\\mathbb{R}^q$, and let $0<t<1$, with $(1-t)S_1+tS_2\\subset S_0$. Let $f_1,f_2,f_0$ be positive continuous functions on a compact box containing the closures of these sets. If $$f_0((1-t)x+ty)\\ge f_1(x)^{1-t}f_2(y)^t\n \\quad(x\\in S_1,\\ y\\in S_2),$$ then $$\\label{eq:integral-interpolation}\n \\int_{S_0}f_0\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_{S_1}f_1\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{S_2}f_2\\right)^t.$$ In particular, for finite unions $S,D_0$ of open boxes in the sampling cube, $$\\label{eq:dense-logconcave-interpolation}\n \\int_{(1-t)S+tD_0}\\rho\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_S\\rho\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{D_0}\\rho\\right)^t.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-115.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-integral-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S_1,S_2,S_0$ be bounded finite unions of open axis-aligned boxes in $\\mathbb{R}^q$, and let $0<t<1$, with $(1-t)S_1+tS_2\\subset S_0$. Let $f_1,f_2,f_0$ be positive continuous functions on a compact box containing the closures of these sets. If $$f_0((1-t)x+ty)\\ge f_1(x)^{1-t}f_2(y)^t\n \\quad(x\\in S_1,\\ y\\in S_2),$$ then $$\\label{eq:integral-interpolation}\n \\int_{S_0}f_0\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_{S_1}f_1\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{S_2}f_2\\right)^t.$$ In particular, for finite unions $S,D_0$ of open boxes in the sampling cube, $$\\label{eq:dense-logconcave-interpolation}\n \\int_{(1-t)S+tD_0}\\rho\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_S\\rho\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{D_0}\\rho\\right)^t.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-115.1"

Let $f_1,f_2,f_0$ be positive continuous functions on a compact box containing the closures of these sets. If $$f_0((1-t)x+ty)\\ge f_1(x)^{1-t}f_2(y)^t\n \\quad(x\\in S_1,\\ y\\in S_2),$$ then $$\\label{eq:integral-interpolation}\n \\int_{S_0}f_0\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_{S_1}f_1\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{S_2}f_2\\right)^t.$$ In particular, for finite unions $S,D_0$ of open boxes in the sampling cube, $$\\label{eq:dense-logconcave-interpolation}\n \\int_{(1-t)S+tD_0}\\rho\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_S\\rho\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{D_0}\\rho\\right)^t.$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixing",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a reversible kernel on a nonempty finite set $V$, with stationary probabilities $\\pi(x)>0$ and $P(x,x)\\ge1/2$. Set $Q(x,y)=\\pi(x)P(x,y)$ and $$\\mathcal E_P(H)=\\sum_{\\{x,y\\}\\subseteq V}\n Q(x,y)(H(x)-H(y))^2,$$ where unordered pairs have distinct endpoints. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\pi H\\le K\\mathcal E_P(H)$ for all $H$, with $K\\ge1$, then, writing $\\pi_{\\min}=\\min_{x\\in V}\\pi(x)$, for every integer $t\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:elementary-mixing}\n \\lVert P^t(x,\\cdot)-\\pi\\rVert_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\le \\pi_{\\min}^{-1}e^{-t/K}.$$ If, for some $\\kappa>0$, every cut satisfies $$Q(A,A^c):=\\sum_{x\\in A,\\,y\\notin A}Q(x,y)\n \\ge\\kappa\\min\\{\\pi(A),\\pi(A^c)\\},$$ then the variance inequality holds with $K=2/\\kappa^2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-115.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-mixing",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a reversible kernel on a nonempty finite set $V$, with stationary probabilities $\\pi(x)>0$ and $P(x,x)\\ge1/2$. If $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Var}}_\\pi H\\le K\\mathcal E_P(H)$ for all $H$, with $K\\ge1$, then, writing $\\pi_{\\min}=\\min_{x\\in V}\\pi(x)$, for every integer $t\\ge0$, $$\\label{eq:elementary-mixing}\n \\lVert P^t(x,\\cdot)-\\pi\\rVert_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\n \\le \\pi_{\\min}^{-1}e^{-t/K}.$$ If, for some $\\kappa>0$, every cut satisfies $$Q(A,A^c):=\\sum_{x\\in A,\\,y\\notin A}Q(x,y)\n \\ge\\kappa\\min\\{\\pi(A),\\pi(A^c)\\},$$ then the variance inequality holds with $K=2/\\kappa^2$.",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Feasible contingency tables** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For nonnegative integer vectors $r=(r_1,\ldots,r_m)$ and $c=(c_1,\ldots,c_n)$ with common total $N$, write $$\Omega(r,c)=\left\{X\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ge0}^{m\times n}: \sum_jX_{ij}=r_i,\quad \sum_iX_{ij}=c_j\right\}.$$
- **Total variation distance** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For probability laws on a finite set, we use the convention $\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\nu)=\tfrac12\sum_x\|\mu(x)-\nu(x)\|$.
- **Large and small slots** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Put $I=\{i:r_i\ge U\}$ and $J=\{j:c_j\ge U\}$. The *large slots* are the cells in the complete rectangle $\mathcal B=I\times J$; the remaining cells form the set $\mathcal S$ of *small slots*.
- **Balanced slot** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A slot is *balanced* when $x_s=q_s$, equivalently $x_s+y_s=B_s$.
- **Feasible state** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A state is *feasible* when all residuals are nonnegative and $R_i^0=0$ if $k_i=0$, $P_j^0=0$ if $\ell_j=0$.
- **Completion fiber and weight** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Write $\mathcal F_X$ for the set of nonnegative integer tables on $\mathcal B$ with margins $R_i(X),P_j(X)$, and set $f(X)=\|\mathcal F_X\|$.
- **Graph masses** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Let $\mathcal X$ be the feasible states, $\mathcal T\subseteq\mathcal X$ the transversals, and $$\label{eq:graph-masses} Z_0=\sum_{X\in\mathcal T}f(X),\qquad \Lambda=\sum_{X\in\mathcal X}f(X),\qquad \pi(X)=\frac{f(X)}{\Lambda}.$$
- **Repair map** ([source](#eq:repair-map)): For a defect with negative slot $s=(i,j)$ and positive slot $t=(i',j')$, put $v=(i',j)$. With $e_w$ denoting a small-slot unit vector, define its repair $\mathcal R(X)$ by $$\label{eq:repair-map} x'=x-e_t+\mathbf{1}_{\{v\in\mathcal S\}}e_v, \qquad q'=x',\qquad y'=U\mathbf{1}-q'.$$
- **Graph energy** ([source](#eq:graph-energy)): Its unnormalized energy is $$\label{eq:graph-energy} E(H)=\sum_{\{X,Y\}\text{ an edge}} \min\{f(X),f(Y)\}(H(X)-H(Y))^2.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-lem-repair** [claim](#stmt-lem-repair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-repair): Repairs a defect injectively into a feasible transversal and preserves the stated ordered prefixes. -> Repairs a defect injectively into a feasible transversal and preserves the stated ordered prefixes.
- **R-lem-small-entry** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-entry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-entry): Bounds the probability of a small cell entry when its incident margins are sufficiently large. -> Bounds the probability of a small cell entry when its incident margins are sufficiently large.
- **R-prop-stationary-success** [claim](#stmt-prop-stationary-success) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stationary-success): Bounds stationary mass and gives conditional uniformity after successful unpadding. -> Bounds stationary mass and gives conditional uniformity after successful unpadding.
- **R-lem-laminar-signature** [claim](#stmt-lem-laminar-signature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-laminar-signature): Controls the positive eigenvalues of the unsummed two-removal matrices. -> Controls the positive eigenvalues of the unsummed two-removal matrices.
- **R-lem-pair-summation** [claim](#stmt-lem-pair-summation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-summation): Preserves the signature under balanced summation and bounds the weighted quadratic evaluation under the balance condition. -> Preserves the signature under balanced summation and bounds the weighted quadratic evaluation under the balance condition.
- **R-prop-signature** [claim](#stmt-prop-signature) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signature): Extends the signature bound to arbitrary box-valid contexts and balanced-slot sums. -> Extends the signature bound to arbitrary box-valid contexts and balanced-slot sums.
- **R-lem-child-logconcavity** [claim](#stmt-lem-child-logconcavity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-child-logconcavity): Gives log-concavity and interval support for positive conditional child masses. -> Gives log-concavity and interval support for positive conditional child masses.
- **R-lem-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transport): Controls adjacent conditional means by unit-exchange energy. -> Controls adjacent conditional means by unit-exchange energy.
- **R-thm-poincare** [claim](#stmt-thm-poincare) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-poincare): Bounds graph variance by energy and proves connectivity. -> Bounds graph variance by energy and proves connectivity.
- **R-thm-dense-draw** [claim](#stmt-thm-dense-draw) / [proof](#pm-h-18): Provides a bounded almost-uniform draw from dense completion blocks, including deterministic degenerate blocks. -> Provides a bounded almost-uniform draw from dense completion blocks, including deterministic degenerate blocks.
- **R-prop-bin-acceptance** [claim](#stmt-prop-bin-acceptance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bin-acceptance): Gives a constant acceptance lower bound and exact uniformity conditional on acceptance for an ideal bin trial. -> Gives a constant acceptance lower bound and exact uniformity conditional on acceptance for an ideal bin trial.
- **R-lem-integral-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-lem-integral-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-integral-interpolation): Bounds the interpolated integral for finite unions of open boxes with positive continuous weights. -> Bounds the interpolated integral for finite unions of open boxes with positive continuous weights.
- **R-lem-mixing** [claim](#stmt-lem-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mixing): Turns variance or conductance bounds into a mixing estimate for lazy reversible finite kernels. -> Turns variance or conductance bounds into a mixing estimate for lazy reversible finite kernels.
- **R-prop-bin-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-bin-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bin-gap): Bounds bin-chain conductance and mixing from any initial bin. -> Bounds bin-chain conductance and mixing from any initial bin.
- **R-prop-small-chain** [claim](#stmt-prop-small-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-small-chain): Bounds edge transitions and mixing of the ideal completion-weighted small chain. -> Bounds edge transitions and mixing of the ideal completion-weighted small chain.
- **R-thm-almost-uniform** [claim](#stmt-thm-almost-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-almost-uniform): Bounds the output error of the implemented finite procedure and its worst-case bit work. -> Bounds the output error of the implemented finite procedure and its worst-case bit work.
- **R-prop-law-tabulation** [claim](#stmt-prop-law-tabulation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-law-tabulation): Tabulates the actual bounded sampler law exactly with a common dyadic denominator and accuracy-independent state spaces. -> Tabulates the actual bounded sampler law exactly with a common dyadic denominator and accuracy-independent state spaces.
- **R-thm-exact-sampling** [claim](#stmt-thm-exact-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-exact-sampling): Provides exactly uniform output with almost-sure termination and polynomial expected bit cost. -> Provides exactly uniform output with almost-sure termination and polynomial expected bit cost.

Put $S(l)=0$ outside $0\\le l\\le D'-1$ and $u_j=0$ outside $1\\le j\\le D'-1$. The entries $u_{j-1}$ and $u_{j+1}$ are the double removals at the first and second fresh coordinates, respectively. If $D'=B+1$, then $$\\label{eq:pair-weighted}\n 2\\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}u_jg_j^2\n \\ \\le\\\n -h^{\\mathsf T}\\left(\\sum_{j=1}^{D'-1}j(D'-j)V_j\\right)h.$$",

Let $f_1,f_2,f_0$ be positive continuous functions on a compact box containing the closures of these sets. If $$f_0((1-t)x+ty)\\ge f_1(x)^{1-t}f_2(y)^t\n \\quad(x\\in S_1,\\ y\\in S_2),$$ then $$\\label{eq:integral-interpolation}\n \\int_{S_0}f_0\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_{S_1}f_1\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{S_2}f_2\\right)^t.$$ In particular, for finite unions $S,D_0$ of open boxes in the sampling cube, $$\\label{eq:dense-logconcave-interpolation}\n \\int_{(1-t)S+tD_0}\\rho\\ge\n       \\left(\\int_S\\rho\\right)^{1-t}\n       \\left(\\int_{D_0}\\rho\\right)^t.$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **residual-weights**: Pointwise domination gives nonnegative residual integer weights with the stated total.
- **uniform-mixture**: Mixing the bounded law and the residual law with the correction probability recovers the uniform law.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **bounded-exact-time**: Treat exact uniform generation as having a polynomial work bound on every execution. -> The rare correction branch performs exhaustive work and includes an unbounded rejection loop.
- **restricted-cells**: Apply the unrestricted sampler directly to tables with cell bounds or forbidden positions. -> The stated target space includes every nonnegative integer matrix with the prescribed margins.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The exact-sampling proof gives a finite constructive integer residual-weight calculation. This code implements that calculation for a supplied dyadic law; it does not implement the bounded table sampler or certify its global running-time proof.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of [R, D, dyadic numerator array].
# PRECONDITIONS: R nonnegative, D positive, nonempty nonnegative integer array summing to 2**R; pointwise domination.
# OUTPUTS: Integer residual weights and their total, or null for a rejected input.
# TIME: Linear in array length arithmetic operations; polynomial in integer bit length.
# SPACE: Linear in array length integers.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact arbitrary precision integers using python-flint.
# FAILURE: Invalid law or negative residual weight returns null; malformed JSON raises an error.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz

def residual(case):
    R, D, numerators = case
    if type(R) is not int or type(D) is not int or R < 0 or D < 1:
        return None
    if not numerators or any(type(v) is not int or v < 0 for v in numerators):
        return None
    unit = fmpz(2)**R
    if sum(numerators) != unit:
        return None
    M = fmpz(len(numerators))
    branch = fmpz(2)**D
    weights = [unit*branch - M*(branch-1)*fmpz(a) for a in numerators]
    if any(w < 0 for w in weights):
        return None
    total = M*unit
    return {'weights': [int(w) for w in weights], 'total': int(total)}

print(json.dumps([residual(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)], separators=(',', ':')))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | strong | implementable | inferred | A sampling component can return feasible integer matrices with prescribed margins and a specified uniformity guarantee. Deployment suitability still depends on the deliberately large polynomial exponents. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Simulation can draw equally weighted feasible fixed-margin arrays when that uniform law is the intended model. The paper supplies no game-specific dynamics or preference-weighted distribution. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The explicit sampling and law-tabulation procedures can underlie a fixed-margin generation tool. Exact tabulation belongs to the rare correction branch and is unsuitable as routine work justified by the ordinary polynomial sampling bound. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper explicitly constructs bounded almost-uniform and almost-surely terminating exact samplers for arbitrary equal-total nonnegative integer margins with both dimensions variable. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The exact-law calculation specifies a cache of completion laws and finite-kernel probability propagation. This is a representation for the correction computation rather than a general-purpose data-structure performance theorem. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source uses a classical random-bit and bit-operation model; accelerator kernels, memory layouts, and hardware speedup guarantees are ABSENT. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source proves uniform sampling of constrained integer arrays. Cryptographic security assumptions, adversarial randomness guarantees, and security reductions are ABSENT. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The target is a uniform law on fixed-margin tables; a learning objective, fitted probabilistic model, and learning performance guarantee are ABSENT. A possible downstream use does not establish a source-supported learning contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The feasible arrays satisfy transportation-style row and column constraints. Uniform generation is directly adaptable to sampling such feasible integer allocations, but the paper does not optimize an objective. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The implemented chain retains a small state and uses fresh completion draws. A distributed execution protocol, coordination invariant, and communication complexity are ABSENT. |
| other (computational_probability) | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Computational probability is directly served by the finite-law propagation and residual-mixture construction, which turn controlled approximation into exact uniform output with an expected bit-cost guarantee. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-1** (hypothesis): The sampling guarantees concern nonnegative integer margins with equal totals in the unbiased-random-bit and bit-operation model. Blocked use: ['Sampling with unequal totals, negative margins, or noninteger margins.', 'Transferring the exact law guarantee to biased random draws without an exact simulation.']
- **limit-2** (counterexample_scope): The feasible family has no cell bounds or forbidden positions; restricted-cell sampling is outside the stated guarantee. Blocked use: ['Applying the unrestricted-table theorem directly to cell bounds or forbidden positions.']
- **limit-3** (approximation): The bounded sampler provides a total-variation approximation, while exactness belongs to the separate correction algorithm. Blocked use: ['Calling the bounded almost-uniform output exactly uniform.']
- **limit-4** (formal_scope): The exact sampler has polynomial expected bit cost and almost-sure termination; a polynomial bound on every execution is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Using the exact algorithm as a guaranteed bounded-latency sampler.']
- **limit-5** (hypothesis): The dense completion routine requires equal-total nonnegative integer margins below the coordinate cap and sufficiently large block margins. Blocked use: ['Calling the dense routine on arbitrary unpadded small-margin blocks.']
- **limit-6** (edge_case): Empty dimensions, zero total, and a singleton dimension use the unique-table construction rather than the general chain. Blocked use: ['Assuming a nontrivial random walk is required for every input.']
- **limit-7** (formal_scope): The signature closure is specific to balanced-pair summation; arbitrary sums with the same individual signature are not covered. Blocked use: ['Replacing balanced-pair summation by arbitrary matrix addition.']
- **limit-8** (formal_scope): The weighted quadratic estimate does not assert the signature of the weighted matrix itself. Blocked use: ['Claiming that the weighted transport matrix has at most one positive eigenvalue.']
- **limit-9** (hypothesis): The finite-chain mixing estimate requires reversibility, positive stationary masses, and laziness. Blocked use: ['Applying the mixing lemma to a nonreversible or nonlazy kernel without a separate argument.']
- **limit-10** (formal_scope): The implemented approximate kernel is controlled by adaptive coupling; its reversibility or stationary-law identity is not asserted by that argument. Blocked use: ['Assigning the ideal stationary law or reversibility to the implemented kernel solely from the coupling guarantee.']
- **limit-11** (formal_scope): Exact law tabulation is exhaustive work reserved for the rare correction branch; its cost does not give polynomial worst-case exact sampling. Blocked use: ['Treating full law tabulation as an ordinary polynomial-cost sampling operation.']
- **limit-12** (hypothesis): Uniformity after unpadding is conditional on a transversal state and completion entries meeting the padding threshold. Blocked use: ['Returning an untested defect or an inadequately padded completion as a uniform original table.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 115 (`lean/docs/115.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Exact Uniform Sampling of Contingency Tables with Arbitrary Margins](../../preprints/Exact-Uniform-Sampling-of-Contingency-Tables-with-Arbitrary-Margins-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
> - [An FPRAS for Cell-Bounded Contingency Tables](../../preprints/An-FPRAS-for-Cell-Bounded-Contingency-Tables-September-24-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization gives an exact uniform sampler for nonnegative integer contingency tables with arbitrary prescribed row and column sums having equal totals. It terminates almost surely, every halted output is feasible, and each table has exactly the uniform limiting probability. Expected bit complexity is polynomial in the dimensions and the binary length of the margins.
>
> It also gives a sampler with a fixed polynomial time bound on every execution whose total-variation error is at most $2^{-k}$ for requested precision $k\ge1$. The same Comparator file includes the companion approximation scheme for counting tables with individual cell bounds.
>
> The formalization gives a fully polynomial randomized approximation scheme for counting nonnegative integer matrices with prescribed row sums, column sums, and individual entry bounds. Both dimensions may vary, the margins and bounds are binary encoded, and zero entry bounds are allowed. For rational $0<\varepsilon,\delta<1$, the algorithm returns a nonnegative estimate with relative error at most $\varepsilon$ with probability at least $1-\delta$, and returns zero on every execution when no table exists.
>
> The running time is polynomial in the encoded input size, $\varepsilon^{-1}$, and $\log(\delta^{-1})$ on every random tape. The same Comparator file also contains the companion sampling results for tables without cell bounds.

Comparator source `ContingencyTables.lean` (9515 bytes, sha256 `809f0a2575fb97e579e883d47aa6f38717c9df57f4b186d21dcdeb59743118b5`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace MatchingFPRAS

abbrev Symbol := Fin 8

/-- Binary numeral followed by a delimiter. Tape blank is 0; bits use 1 and 2. -/
def encodeNat (n : ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  (Nat.bits n).map (fun b => if b then 2 else 1) ++ [3]

def encodeInt (z : ℤ) : List Symbol :=
  (if z < 0 then [5] else [4]) ++ encodeNat z.natAbs

/-- A rational encoded by its reduced numerator and positive denominator. -/
def encodeRat (q : ℚ) : List Symbol := encodeInt q.num ++ encodeNat q.den

/-- A uniform randomized Post-Turing machine: a single finite transition table. -/
structure RandomMachine where
  states : ℕ
  transition : Fin (states + 1) → Symbol → Bool →
    Option (Fin (states + 1) × Turing.TM0.Stmt Symbol)

structure Configuration (A : RandomMachine) where
  state : Option (Fin (A.states + 1))
  tape : Turing.Tape Symbol

/-- A halted configuration is absorbing. Each nonhalting tick is exactly one
write or one move, or the detection of halt. -/
def tick (A : RandomMachine) (c : Configuration A) (bit : Bool) : Configuration A :=
  match c.state with
  | none => c
  | some s => match A.transition s c.tape.head bit with
    | none => ⟨none, c.tape⟩
    | some (s', action) =>
      ⟨some s', match action with
        | .move d => c.tape.move d
        | .write a => c.tape.write a⟩

def initial (A : RandomMachine) (input : List Symbol) : Configuration A :=
  ⟨some 0, Turing.Tape.mk₁ input⟩

/-- Bounded execution on a finite prefix of the random tape. -/
def run (A : RandomMachine) (input : List Symbol) {t : ℕ}
    (bits : Fin t → Bool) : Configuration A :=
  (List.ofFn bits).foldl (tick A) (initial A input)

def Outputs (A : RandomMachine) (input : List Symbol) {t : ℕ}
    (bits : Fin t → Bool) (q : ℚ) : Prop :=
  (run A input bits).state = none ∧
    (run A input bits).tape.right₀ = Turing.ListBlank.mk (encodeRat q)

end MatchingFPRAS

namespace ContingencyTables

open Finset
variable {I J : Type*} [Fintype I] [Fintype J]

def HasMargins (X : I → J → ℕ) (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (∀ i, ∑ j, X i j = r i) ∧ (∀ j, ∑ i, X i j = c j)

def Table (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) := {X : I → J → ℕ // HasMargins X r c}

def BoundedTable (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) (b : I → J → ℕ) :=
  {X : Table r c // ∀ i j, X.val i j ≤ b i j}

theorem entry_le_row {X : I → J → ℕ} {r : I → ℕ} {c : J → ℕ}
    (h : HasMargins X r c) (i : I) (j : J) : X i j ≤ r i := by
  rw [← h.1 i]
  exact single_le_sum (fun _ _ => Nat.zero_le _) (mem_univ j)

theorem entry_le_total {X : I → J → ℕ} {r : I → ℕ} {c : J → ℕ}
    (h : HasMargins X r c) (i : I) (j : J) : X i j ≤ ∑ i, r i :=
  (entry_le_row h i j).trans (single_le_sum (fun _ _ => Nat.zero_le _) (mem_univ i))

instance table_finite (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) : Finite (Table r c) := by
  let encode : Table r c → I → J → Fin ((∑ i, r i) + 1) :=
    fun X i j => ⟨X.val i j, Nat.lt_succ_of_le (entry_le_total X.property i j)⟩
  apply Finite.of_injective encode
  intro X Y h
  apply Subtype.ext
  funext i j
  exact congrArg Fin.val (congrFun (congrFun h i) j)

noncomputable instance table_fintype (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) : Fintype (Table r c) :=
  Fintype.ofFinite _

noncomputable instance boundedTable_fintype (r : I → ℕ) (c : J → ℕ) (b : I → J → ℕ) :
    Fintype (BoundedTable r c b) := by
  classical
  unfold BoundedTable
  infer_instance

end ContingencyTables

namespace ContingencyTables.Algorithms

open scoped BigOperators
open Filter

abbrev Machine := MatchingFPRAS.RandomMachine
abbrev Symbol := MatchingFPRAS.Symbol

def encodeMatrix {m n : ℕ} (X : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  ((List.ofFn fun i => (List.ofFn fun j => MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat (X i j)).flatten)).flatten

def encodeMargins {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat m ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat n ++
    (List.ofFn fun i => MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat (r i)).flatten ++
    (List.ofFn fun j => MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat (c j)).flatten

def encodeSamplingInput {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) (k : ℕ) : List Symbol :=
  encodeMargins r c ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeNat k

def encodeCountingInput {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ)
    (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) (ε δ : ℚ) : List Symbol :=
  encodeMargins r c ++ encodeMatrix b ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeRat ε ++ MatchingFPRAS.encodeRat δ

/-- The tape alphabet has positive cardinality. -/
theorem alphabetSize_neZero : NeZero (8 : ℕ) := inferInstance

/-- A halted tape contains the row-major binary matrix encoding. -/
def OutputsTable (A : Machine) (input : List Symbol) {t m n : ℕ}
    (bits : Fin t → Bool) (X : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : Prop :=
  (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).state = none ∧
    @Turing.Tape.right₀ MatchingFPRAS.Symbol (@Fin.instInhabited 8 alphabetSize_neZero)
      (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).tape =
    @Turing.ListBlank.mk Symbol (@Fin.instInhabited 8 alphabetSize_neZero) (encodeMatrix X)

noncomputable def tableMass (A : Machine) (input : List Symbol) (t : ℕ)
    {m n : ℕ} (X : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact ((Finset.univ.filter fun bits : Fin t → Bool => OutputsTable A input bits X).card : ℝ) /
    (2 : ℝ) ^ t

noncomputable def haltMass (A : Machine) (input : List Symbol) (t : ℕ) : ℝ := by
  classical
  exact ((Finset.univ.filter fun bits : Fin t → Bool =>
      (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).state = none).card : ℝ) / (2 : ℝ) ^ t

def samplingSize (n : ℕ) {m : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (k : ℕ) : ℕ :=
  m + n + Nat.clog 2 ((∑ i, r i) + 1) + k + 1

/-- Paper 200-01, Theorem main(i): bounded runtime on every random tape
and total variation at most `2⁻ᵏ`, with freely varying dimensions. -/
def BoundedSamplingStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Machine) (C d : ℕ), 0 < C ∧
    ∀ (m n : ℕ) (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ),
      (∑ i, r i) = ∑ j, c j → ∀ k : ℕ, 1 ≤ k →
        let input := encodeSamplingInput r c k
        let t := C * (samplingSize n r k) ^ d
        (∀ bits : Fin t → Bool, ∃ X : Table r c, OutputsTable A input bits X.val) ∧
        (∑ X : Table r c,
          |tableMass A input t X.val - (Fintype.card (Table r c) : ℝ)⁻¹|) / 2 ≤
            ((2 : ℝ) ^ k)⁻¹

/-- Paper 200-01, Theorem main(ii): every halted output is feasible, each
table has limiting mass `1/|Ω|`, termination has probability one, and the
expected number of bit operations is bounded by a uniform polynomial. -/
def ExactSamplingStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Machine) (C d : ℕ), 0 < C ∧
    ∀ (m n : ℕ) (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ),
      (∑ i, r i) = ∑ j, c j →
        let input := encodeMargins r c
        let tail : ℕ → ℝ := fun t => 1 - haltMass A input t
        (∀ t (bits : Fin t → Bool), (MatchingFPRAS.run A input bits).state = none →
          ∃ X : Table r c, OutputsTable A input bits X.val) ∧
        (∀ X : Table r c, Tendsto (fun t => tableMass A input t X.val) atTop
          (nhds ((Fintype.card (Table r c) : ℝ)⁻¹))) ∧
        Tendsto (haltMass A input) atTop (nhds 1) ∧
        Summable tail ∧
        tsum tail ≤ C * (samplingSize n r 0) ^ d

noncomputable def count {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ)
    (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) : ℕ := Fintype.card (BoundedTable r c b)

def countingTime (C d : ℕ) {m n : ℕ} (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ)
    (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ) (ε δ : ℚ) : ℕ :=
  C * ((encodeCountingInput r c b ε δ).length + ⌈ε⁻¹⌉₊ +
    Nat.clog 2 ⌈δ⁻¹⌉₊ + 1) ^ d

noncomputable def goodCountingTapes (A : Machine) {m n : ℕ}
    (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ)
    (ε δ : ℚ) (t : ℕ) : Finset (Fin t → Bool) := by
  classical
  exact Finset.univ.filter fun bits => ∃ q : ℚ,
    MatchingFPRAS.Outputs A (encodeCountingInput r c b ε δ) bits q ∧
      (1 - ε) * count r c b ≤ q ∧ q ≤ (1 + ε) * count r c b

/-- Paper 200-02, Theorem main. The input length includes the rational
accuracy parameters, and infeasible inputs always produce exactly zero. -/
def CountingStatement : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Machine) (C d : ℕ), 0 < C ∧
    ∀ (m n : ℕ), 0 < m → 0 < n →
    ∀ (r : Fin m → ℕ) (c : Fin n → ℕ) (b : Fin m → Fin n → ℕ),
      (∑ i, r i) = ∑ j, c j →
      ∀ ε δ : ℚ, 0 < ε → ε < 1 → 0 < δ → δ < 1 →
        let input := encodeCountingInput r c b ε δ
        let t := countingTime C d r c b ε δ
        (∀ bits : Fin t → Bool, ∃ q : ℚ, 0 ≤ q ∧ MatchingFPRAS.Outputs A input bits q) ∧
        (count r c b = 0 → ∀ bits : Fin t → Bool, MatchingFPRAS.Outputs A input bits 0) ∧
        ((goodCountingTapes A r c b ε δ t).card : ℚ) / (2 : ℚ) ^ t ≥ 1 - δ

end ContingencyTables.Algorithms

namespace ContingencyTables

/-- An almost-uniform sampler with polynomial cost on every execution. -/
theorem boundedSampling : Algorithms.BoundedSamplingStatement := by sorry

/-- Exact uniform sampling in polynomial expected bit time. -/
theorem exactSampling : Algorithms.ExactSamplingStatement := by sorry

/-- A cell-bounded FPRAS, with zero output on every infeasible execution. -/
theorem counting : Algorithms.CountingStatement := by sorry

end ContingencyTables

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give an exact uniform sampler for nonnegative integer contingency tables with arbitrary prescribed margins. It terminates almost surely and has expected bit complexity polynomial in both dimensions and the binary length of the margins. No positivity, balance, sparsity, or fixed-dimension assumption is required.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For nonnegative integer vectors $r=(r_1,\ldots,r_m)$ and $c=(c_1,\ldots,c_n)$ with common total $N$, write $$\Omega(r,c)=\left\{X\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ge0}^{m\times n}:
       \sum_jX_{ij}=r_i,\quad \sum_iX_{ij}=c_j\right\}.$$ The uniform distribution on this finite set gives every table equal mass. We study sampling when both dimensions vary and the margins are encoded in binary. In particular, a polynomial bound in the numeric total $N$ would not, in general, give a polynomial bound in the input length. There are no cell bounds or forbidden positions: every nonnegative integer matrix with the prescribed margins is allowed. For probability laws on a finite set, we use the convention $\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(\mu,\nu)=\tfrac12\sum_x|\mu(x)-\nu(x)|$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For arbitrary nonnegative integer margins of equal total, the following algorithms exist in the model of unbiased random bits and bit operations.*

1.  *Given an integer $k\ge1$, a bounded-time algorithm outputs a table in $\Omega(r,c)$ whose law is within $2^{-k}$ in total variation of the uniform law. Its running time is polynomial in $m,n,\log(N+1),k$.*

2.  *An algorithm outputs an exactly uniform table in $\Omega(r,c)$, terminates almost surely, and has expected running time polynomial in $m,n,\log(N+1)$.*

*The polynomials are uniform over all margin vectors. No positivity, sparsity, balance, or fixed-dimension hypothesis is imposed.*

</div>

For a rational tolerance $0<\varepsilon<1$ encoded by a binary numerator and denominator, integer comparisons compute $k=\lceil\log_2(1/\varepsilon)\rceil$ in time polynomial in that encoding length. Thereafter part <span class="upright">(i)</span> has accuracy dependence polynomial in $\log(1/\varepsilon)$. The algorithms below are explicit, although their exponents are deliberately large. Part <span class="upright">(ii)</span> concerns expected time: an exceptionally rare branch performs exhaustive computation.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
###### Counting and sampling fixed-margin arrays.

Contingency tables are a basic family of integer arrays with linear constraints. Their enumeration and exact and approximate generation are treated, for example, by Diaconis and Gangolli (Diaconis and Gangolli 1995) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[DiaconisGangolli1995](#ref-DiaconisGangolli1995)]. The algorithmic question involves more than producing one feasible table: the law must give all feasible tables nearly or exactly the same mass, even when their number and the coordinate ranges are exponentially large in the margin bit length.

For two rows, Dyer and Greenhill (Dyer and Greenhill 2000) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[DyerGreenhill2000](#ref-DyerGreenhill2000)] give polynomial-time approximate counting and sampling. Their heat-bath chain has mixing time at most $n(n-1)\log(N/\varepsilon)/2$ in the author version’s Theorem 4.1. Dependence on $\log N$ rather than $N$ makes this a polynomial bound in the binary input length. Kijima and Matsui (Kijima and Matsui 2003, Theorems 3.1 and 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KM2003](#ref-KM2003)] give exact uniform sampling for two rows by monotone coupling from the past, after sorting the columns. Their expected $O(n^3\ln N)$ bound is stated for pseudocode using uniform real draws. These results allow arbitrary two-row margin sizes after deleting zero margins.

The fixed-row case extends beyond two rows. Cryan and Dyer (Cryan and Dyer 2003) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[CD2003](#ref-CD2003)] combine dynamic programming with volume estimation for approximate counting and sampling. Dyer (Dyer 2003, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Dyer2003](#ref-Dyer2003)] gives a dynamic-programming rejection sampler that is exactly uniform, analyzed in arithmetic operations. Cryan, Dyer, Goldberg, Jerrum, and Martin (Cryan et al. 2006) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CDGJM2006](#ref-CDGJM2006)] prove rapid mixing of the $2\times2$ heat-bath chain for every fixed number of rows. The polynomial bounds in these results depend on that fixed row count; they do not give a bound polynomial jointly in both dimensions.

DeSalvo and Zhao (DeSalvo and Zhao 2016, Theorem 2.3 and Remarks 2.4–2.5) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[DZ2016](#ref-DZ2016)] give a general exact divide-and-conquer construction using $O(mn\log M)$ expected random bits, where $M$ is the largest margin. In the cited arXiv version, their polynomial total-runtime conclusion is conditional on efficient evaluation of the parity-restricted counting quantities in Conjecture 2.6; the bit-time bounds in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> are unconditional.

Another line of work uses the geometry of the transportation polytope when margins are large. Dyer, Kannan, and Mount (Dyer, Kannan, and Mount 1997) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DKM1997](#ref-DKM1997)] develop scaled coordinates, softened constraints, lattice walks, and rounding comparisons for dense tables. Morris (Morris 2002) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Morris2002](#ref-Morris2002)] improves the dense sampling bounds through expanded polytopes and rounding. These methods are close antecedents of the dense completion routine below. Our use of that routine is conditional: a padding construction makes every completion problem dense, while a separate chain handles all cells incident to small margins.

For sparse margins, Arman, Gao, and Wormald (Arman, Gao, and Wormald 2021, Theorem 1 and Remark 2) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[AGW2021](#ref-AGW2021)] give exact uniform generation under $5\Delta^4<N$, where $\Delta$ is the largest margin, in expected $O(N)$ time with unit-cost arithmetic on integers of magnitude $O(N)$. The condition is a genuine restriction, although within this family it also bounds $N$ polynomially in the dimensions. Their 2021 introduction surveys fixed-row, dense, and sparse algorithms and reports that no polynomial-time approximately uniform sampler for arbitrary margins was then known. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> removes those margin and dimension restrictions and includes all random bits and integer arithmetic in its cost.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
###### Approximation and exact correction.

Exactness and bounded running time are separate issues. Our approximate algorithm has a deterministic polynomial work bound for each accuracy request. The final exact correction uses the residual-mixture construction of Göbel, Liu, Manurangsi, and Pappik (Göbel et al. 2024, sec. 3.1, Algorithm 1 and Theorem 6) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[GLMP2024](#ref-GLMP2024)]. That construction combines a sufficiently accurate sampler with a rare exhaustive draw from the remaining probability mass. Its application here requires an additional property: the actual implemented output law can be tabulated in time with an exponential input-size factor but only polynomial dependence on the accuracy bits. We prove that property and give the dyadic implementation explicitly. Thus the exact algorithm has polynomial expected bit cost, not a polynomial bound on every execution.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Proof strategy.

Only cells incident to a margin below a polynomial threshold are explicit coordinates of the auxiliary chain. The remaining cells form a complete rectangle. We pad this rectangle so every conditional completion problem has large margins. Each explicit cell has two bounded integer values, one for its row and one for its column. Besides states where these values agree, called transversals, we allow a positive unit discrepancy at one cell and a negative unit discrepancy at another.

The target law on these small states assigns weight equal to the number of padded completions. Sampling a state from that law and then a uniform completion makes all state/completion pairs equally likely. Accept only a transversal whose large completion entries are all at least the padding. Subtracting it then gives a uniform original table conditional on acceptance. Section <a href="#sec:graph" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:graph">2</a> constructs a repair map for the defect states and uses it to bound their weight and the probability of successful unpadding. The bounded sampler never evaluates completion counts: an ideal transition uses a uniform completion draw to test a proposed move.

To prove that this chain mixes in polynomial time, we expose the small-cell values of a transversal in a fixed order. After fixing a prefix, we compare the averages of any real function on the state graph over the two fibers that assign adjacent values to the next cell. The comparison comes from quadratic forms indexed by pairs of unit removals. Section <a href="#sec:signatures" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:signatures">3</a> proves that these forms have at most one positive eigenvalue and that the property survives summing out a balanced pair. A weighted telescoping calculation then bounds the energy of a signed flow between neighboring conditional fibers. Section <a href="#sec:transport" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transport">4</a> combines this bound with conditional-variance decomposition and repair to obtain a Poincaré inequality for the completion-count law. This supplies the small chain’s mixing bound.

Section <a href="#sec:dense" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:dense">5</a> implements the completion draws approximately on a finite scaled grid, using rational arithmetic and dyadic random choices. A continuous log-concave density serves only as an analytic tool for the rejection and conductance bounds. Large coordinate ranges appear in finite sums in the proof but are never enumerated in an ordinary run. Section <a href="#sec:algorithms" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:algorithms">6</a> couples each implemented completion draw to its ideal counterpart and bounds the number of sampling and unpadding attempts, giving the bounded almost-uniform algorithm. For exactness, it also shows how to tabulate that algorithm’s actual finite law, including its stopping rules and fallbacks. This exhaustive computation is performed only on the rare correction branch.

<a id="sec:graph"></a>
### The binary model and the padded graph

This section separates bounded small coordinates from a complete rectangle of large coordinates. Its repair map controls the mass of defect states; padding then gives a uniform lower bound on the chance of recovering an original table from a stationary state and its completion.

<a id="eq:parameters"></a>
We count bit operations and use independent unbiased random bits. All margins are nonnegative integers given in binary, with common total $N$. Accuracy is specified by an integer $k\ge1$, meaning error at most $2^{-k}$. An empty dimension forces $N=0$ and gives a unique empty table. If $N=0$, or if one dimension is one, the unique table can likewise be returned directly. In all remaining constructions the dimensions are positive, and we use $$\label{eq:parameters}
\begin{gathered}
 d=10+(m+1)(n+1),\qquad L=d^{12},\qquad U=d^{20},\\
 C=N+dL+U+2,\qquad b=d+\lceil\log_2(C+2)\rceil.
\end{gathered}$$ Here $d$ is a dimension allowance, $L$ the padding, $U$ the small-margin threshold, $C$ a coordinate cap, and $b$ a bit-size allowance. Thus $d\ge14$, $mn<d$, $U<C$, and $b=O(d+\log(N+1))$. Polynomial bounds in $d,b,k$ have the required binary-input dependence. A fixed feasible table is obtained greedily: choose a row and column of positive remaining margins, place the minimum of those two margins in their cell, and subtract it from both margins. Each placement exhausts a row or column. This uses at most $m+n-1$ positive placements and polynomial bit work, including initialization of the output. We fix index order for this construction and all later ties.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Small states and large completions

Put $I=\{i:r_i\ge U\}$ and $J=\{j:c_j\ge U\}$. The *large slots* are the cells in the complete rectangle $\mathcal B=I\times J$; the remaining cells form the set $\mathcal S$ of *small slots*. Set $B_s=C$ on $\mathcal B$ and $B_s=U$ on $\mathcal S$. Associate to each slot two coordinates $x_s,y_s\in\{0,\ldots,B_s\}$, and put $q_s=B_s-y_s$. Here $x$ is the row viewpoint and $q$ is the column viewpoint. A slot is *balanced* when $x_s=q_s$, equivalently $x_s+y_s=B_s$. The balanced representation of a value $a\in\{0,\ldots,B_s\}$ is $(x_s,y_s)=(a,B_s-a)$; both $x_s$ and $q_s$ then equal $a$.

A graph state retains only the coordinates on $\mathcal S$. Its allowed pattern is either a *transversal*, with all slots balanced, or a *defect*, with distinct slots $t,s$ satisfying $$x_t=q_t+1,\qquad x_s=q_s-1,
 \qquad x_v=q_v\quad(v\notin\{s,t\}).$$ We call $t$ positive and $s$ negative. Both patterns have total doubled occupancy $\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}(x_s+y_s)=U|\mathcal S|$. Define the original residual margins by $$R_i^0(X)=r_i-\sum_{j:(i,j)\in\mathcal S}x_{ij},\qquad
 P_j^0(X)=c_j-\sum_{i:(i,j)\in\mathcal S}q_{ij}.$$ Their totals agree because $\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}(x_s-q_s)=0$. Let $k_i$ and $\ell_j$ be the numbers of large slots in row $i$ and column $j$. A state is *feasible* when all residuals are nonnegative and $R_i^0=0$ if $k_i=0$, $P_j^0=0$ if $\ell_j=0$. Its padded residuals and enlarged full margins are $$\begin{aligned}
 R_i(X)&=R_i^0(X)+k_iL,& P_j(X)&=P_j^0(X)+\ell_jL,\\
 \bar r_i&=r_i+k_iL,&\bar c_j&=c_j+\ell_jL.
\end{aligned}$$ Write $\mathcal F_X$ for the set of nonnegative integer tables on $\mathcal B$ with margins $R_i(X),P_j(X)$, and set $f(X)=|\mathcal F_X|$. On infeasible states set $f(X)=0$. The complete rectangle admits a greedy completion whenever these residual tests hold. If $\mathcal B$ is empty the only completion is the empty table, so feasible states have weight one.

Every completion has total at most $N+|\mathcal B|L<C$, and every nonempty block margin is at least $L$. In particular the formal large capacity $C$ excludes no completion: its doubled coordinates can be taken as $x_s=A_s,y_s=C-A_s$ for $A\in\mathcal F_X$. The bounded sampler will draw completions of this block without enumerating its large coordinate ranges. One-row or one-column blocks have a unique completion and need no random draw.

Let $\mathcal X$ be the feasible states, $\mathcal T\subseteq\mathcal X$ the transversals, and <a id="eq:graph-masses"></a>$$\label{eq:graph-masses}
 Z_0=\sum_{X\in\mathcal T}f(X),\qquad
 \Lambda=\sum_{X\in\mathcal X}f(X),\qquad
 \pi(X)=\frac{f(X)}{\Lambda}.$$ An original feasible table gives a transversal and a completion by retaining its small entries and adding $L$ to each large entry. The small entries respect their capacity because every small cell has an incident original margin below $U$. This construction also gives a specified starting transversal, using the greedy original table.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Repairs and graph edges

<a id="eq:repair-map"></a>
<a id="eq:graph-energy"></a>
For a defect with negative slot $s=(i,j)$ and positive slot $t=(i',j')$, put $v=(i',j)$. With $e_w$ denoting a small-slot unit vector, define its repair $\mathcal R(X)$ by $$\label{eq:repair-map}
 x'=x-e_t+\mathbf{1}_{\{v\in\mathcal S\}}e_v,
 \qquad q'=x',\qquad y'=U\mathbf{1}-q'.$$ The term involving $e_v$ is omitted if $v$ is large. The undirected graph on $\mathcal X$ contains all pairs that differ by lowering one doubled small coordinate by one and raising another by one, and also every pair $\{X,\mathcal R(X)\}$ with $X$ a defect. Repeated edges are counted only once. Its unnormalized energy is $$\label{eq:graph-energy}
 E(H)=\sum_{\{X,Y\}\text{ an edge}}
 \min\{f(X),f(Y)\}(H(X)-H(Y))^2.$$

<a id="stmt-lem-repair"></a>
<div id="lem:repair" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Repair and preservation of prefixes). *The repair is a feasible transversal. For each fixed ordered pair $(s,t)$ it is injective on small states and extends to an injection $$(X,A)\longmapsto
 \begin{cases}
 (\mathcal R(X),A),&v\in\mathcal S,\\
 (\mathcal R(X),A+e_v),&v\in\mathcal B
 \end{cases}
 \quad(A\in\mathcal F_X).$$ Thus $f(X)\le f(\mathcal R(X))$. Order small slots by row and then by column. If $t$ follows $s$, the repair leaves every slot preceding $s$ unchanged. When additionally $x_s=l-1,q_s=l$, its repaired value at $s$ is $l$ if $i'=i$ and $l-1$ otherwise.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-repair"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-repair</summary>

*Proof.* The defect relation is $q=x-e_t+e_s$, and $x_t\ge1$. If $v$ is small, the changes to $x$ lie in row $i'$, with zero total; the changes to $q$ are $q'-q=e_v-e_s$, in column $j$, also with zero total. Hence all residual margins are unchanged. The new common entries are nonnegative. Their row totals are the old $x$ row totals and their column totals are the old $q$ column totals. At each small slot, one of these totals is bounded by an original margin strictly below $U$, proving the capacity constraint. If $j'=j$, then $v=t$ and the two changes to $x$ cancel; the reset of $q$ is confined to that column. If $i'=i$, then $v=s$: the transfer increments the negative slot, decrements the positive slot, and leaves $q$ unchanged. These include both possible coincidences.

If $v$ is large, it differs from $s,t$, and $x'=x-e_t$, $q'=q-e_s$. These vectors remain nonnegative and within capacity, since $x_t\ge1$ and $q_s=x_s+1\ge1$. Only the residuals of row $i'$ and column $j$ increase, each by one. Both lines meet the large block. Adding one to completion entry $v$ therefore gives a valid target completion. Its entries remain below $C$ by the total bound already proved.

For fixed $s,t$, the repaired vector recovers $x=x'+e_t-\mathbf{1}_{\{v\in\mathcal S\}}e_v$, followed by $q=x-e_t+e_s$ and $y=U\mathbf{1}-q$. In the large-receiver case subtract $e_v$ from the target completion; otherwise retain it. These inverses on the image prove both injections.

Finally, $t>s$ implies either $i'=i,j'>j$, in which case $v=s$, or $i'>i$, in which case every small receiver $v$ follows $s$. Only $s,t,v$ can change, proving prefix preservation and the claimed special value. ◻

</details>

For later use, fix a balanced prefix $\sigma$ before $s$, and let $z_-,z_+$ be the total transversal weights extending it with special values $x_s=l-1,l$, respectively. Let $c_{st,l}^{\sigma}$ be the total weight of defects extending that prefix with negative $s$ satisfying $x_s=l-1,q_s=l$ and positive $t>s$. Lemma <a href="#lem:repair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:repair">2</a> maps this entire class injectively, with completions, into one fixed child. Hence <a id="eq:conditional-repair"></a>$$\label{eq:conditional-repair}
 c_{st,l}^{\sigma}\le
 \begin{cases}z_+,&i'=i,\\z_-,&i'\ne i,\end{cases}
 \qquad\text{and in particular}\qquad
 c_{st,l}^{\sigma}\le\max\{z_-,z_+\}.$$ Figure <a href="#fig:repair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:repair">1</a> illustrates the large-receiver case.

<figure id="fig:repair">

<figcaption>The repair when the receiving cell $(i′,j)$ is large. Small cells display $(x,q)$, and the shaded cell displays a completion entry. Decreasing $x_{t}$ and matching the viewpoints increases the residual row margin at $i′$ and column margin at $j$ by one; adding one to $z$ gives an injective map of completions. The other cells remain unchanged.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-repair"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:repair`)

```latex
 every path/.style={line width=.55pt}]

  \fill[blue!9] (2.4,-2.24) rectangle (4.8,-1.12);
```

Visual witness description of PDF page 6 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
Two $2 \times 2$ grid tables side by side, connected by a horizontal rightward arrow.
Left table labeled 'Defect' above. Column headers are '$c_{j'} < U$' and '$c_j \ge U$'. Row headers are '$r_i < U$' and '$r_{i'} \ge U$'. Top-left cell contains 'unchanged'. Top-right cell contains '$s : (a, a + 1)$' above 'negative'. Bottom-left cell contains '$t : (b + 1, b)$' above 'positive'. Bottom-right cell is shaded gray and contains '$z$' above 'large entry'.
```

There are fewer than $4d^2$ ordered unit exchanges. A defect has one designated repair; a transversal has at most $d^2$ inverse repairs, one for each ordered defect type. Thus the degree is at most $5d^2+1$. Neighbors can be listed with polynomial bit work using <a href="#eq:repair-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:repair-map">[eq:repair-map]</a> and its inverse, testing the boxes, patterns and residuals, and removing duplicates. No completion count is needed. On a repair edge the weight in <a href="#eq:graph-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-energy">[eq:graph-energy]</a> equals the source defect’s weight.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Stationary mass and unpadding

The repair bounds defect mass by transversal mass. To recover an original table from a padded completion, we also need to control the probability that a large entry is smaller than the padding. The following estimate applies to unrestricted tables with any prescribed margins.

<a id="stmt-lem-small-entry"></a>
<div id="lem:small-entry" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Small entries). *Consider the uniform law on all nonnegative integer tables with prescribed margins, with at most $m$ rows and $n$ columns and no entry restrictions. If both margins incident to a cell are at least $a\ge4d$, then, for every integer $t$ with $0<t\le a/2$, $$\mathbb{P}\{\text{the cell's entry is less than }t\}\le\frac{4td^3}{a}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-small-entry"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-small-entry</summary>

*Proof.* Let the marked cell be $(i,j)$ and its entry be $h<t$. The other entries in its row have sum at least $a/2$, as do the other entries in its column. Unless this event is empty, choose deterministically $j'\ne j$ and $i'\ne i$ whose donor entries are both at least $a/(2d)$. Such choices exist because each line has fewer than $d$ entries. If there is only one row or column, the event is empty instead. For every $v=1,\ldots,\lfloor a/(2d)\rfloor$, subtract $v$ from the entries $(i,j')$ and $(i',j)$ and add $v$ at $(i,j)$ and $(i',j')$. The resulting table is nonnegative with the same margins. Given this table and the labels $(i',j',h)$, its marked entry determines $v$, and reversing the four changes recovers the source. There are at most $td^2$ possible labels for each output. Since $\lfloor a/(2d)\rfloor\ge a/(4d)$, double counting these moves gives the asserted bound. No upper entry bound is imposed in this argument. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-stationary-success"></a>
<div id="prop:stationary-success" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:unpadding-success"></a>
**Proposition 4** (Stationary mass and unpadding). *The masses satisfy <a id="eq:stationary-mass-bounds"></a>$$\label{eq:stationary-mass-bounds}
 0<Z_0\le\Lambda\le(1+d^2)Z_0,
 \qquad \pi_{\min}\ge(C+1)^{-2d}.$$ Draw $X$ with law $\pi$ and then a uniform $A\in\mathcal F_X$. Declare success if $X$ is a transversal and every large entry of $A$ is at least $L$. Subtracting $L$ from these entries gives a uniform original table conditional on success, and $$\label{eq:unpadding-success}
 \mathbb{P}\{\text{success}\}\ge\frac{1}{2(1+d^2)}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-stationary-success"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-stationary-success</summary>

*Proof.* For each of at most $|\mathcal S|(|\mathcal S|-1)\le d^2$ defect types, the joint injection in Lemma <a href="#lem:repair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:repair">2</a> bounds its total weight by $Z_0$. This proves the upper bound on $\Lambda$; the other mass inequalities follow from inclusion and the greedy starting table. There are $\Lambda$ joint objects $(X,A)$, each specified by $2|\mathcal S|+|\mathcal B|\le2mn<2d$ integer coordinates in $[0,C]$. Thus $\Lambda\le(C+1)^{2d}$, and the positive integer weights $f(X)$ give the minimum-mass bound.

Consider all unrestricted tables with the enlarged margins $\bar r,\bar c$, and call their number $F$. Every transversal and completion gives one such table, so $Z_0\le F$. Each large cell has both enlarged margins at least $U$. Lemma <a href="#lem:small-entry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:small-entry">3</a> and a union bound show that the proportion of these enlarged tables with some large entry below $L$ is at most $$|\mathcal B|\frac{4Ld^3}{U}\le\frac{4Ld^4}{U}
 =\frac4{d^4}<\frac12.$$ Here $L\le U/2$ and $U\ge4d$; an empty block has no bad cells. Every enlarged table passing this test can be unpadded to an original table. Conversely, padding every original table gives exactly one passing table and a feasible transversal with its completion. Consequently the successful pairs are in bijection with $\Omega(r,c)$ and number at least $F/2\ge Z_0/2$. Under the stated joint law every pair has probability $(f(X)/\Lambda)(1/f(X))=1/\Lambda$, proving conditional uniformity and <a href="#eq:unpadding-success" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:unpadding-success">[eq:unpadding-success]</a>. ◻

</details>

If there are no small slots, $\mathcal X$ has one state and all graph energies vanish; the completion and unpadding step still applies. If there is no large block, the completion step is deterministic. These conventions will also be used for the Markov chains below.

<a id="sec:signatures"></a>
### Quadratic signatures and balanced summation

The transport construction uses quadratic forms with at most one positive eigenvalue. We prove the required property for integer coordinates and then show that it survives summation over balanced slots. Integer coordinates allow two units to be removed at the same location, so the diagonal entries and the endpoints of the summation require explicit treatment. Large capacities occur only in finite mathematical sums in this section; the algorithm will not enumerate those sums. The one-positive-eigenvalue viewpoint is central to the theory of Lorentzian polynomials developed by Brändén and Huh (Brändén and Huh 2020) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BH2020](#ref-BH2020)]. Here the needed closure is a discrete balanced-pair summation, established by the laminar decomposition and telescoping calculation below.

<a id="stmt-def-soft-weight"></a>
<div id="def:soft-weight" class="definition">

**Definition 5** (Softened weights). Let $\mathcal B$ and $\mathcal S$ be the large and small slots from Section <a href="#sec:graph" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:graph">2</a>, and put $$T=\sum_s B_s,\qquad
 \mathcal Q=\prod_s\{0,\ldots,B_s\}^2.$$ For a full coordinate vector $\xi=(x_s,y_s)_s$, write $|\xi|=\sum_s(x_s+y_s)$ and $q_s=B_s-y_s$. For $a\in\mathbb{R}$, let $a_+=\max(a,0)$. Define $$\begin{aligned}
 D(\xi)={}&\sum_i\left|\sum_jx_{ij}-\bar r_i\right|
       +\sum_j\left|\sum_iq_{ij}-\bar c_j\right|\\
 &+\sum_i\left(\sum_{j:(i,j)\in\mathcal S}x_{ij}-r_i\right)_+
       +\sum_j\left(\sum_{i:(i,j)\in\mathcal S}q_{ij}-c_j\right)_+.
\end{aligned}$$ For $0<\eta<1$, set $$F_\eta(\xi)=
 \begin{cases}
 \eta^{D(\xi)},&\xi\in\mathcal Q,\quad |\xi|=T,\\
 0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}$$ For a small-coordinate configuration $X$ of total $\sum_{s\in\mathcal S}B_s$, define <a id="eq:soft-marginal"></a>$$\label{eq:soft-marginal}
 f_\eta(X)=
 \sum_{\substack{(x_s,y_s)=(a_s,B_s-a_s),\ 0\le a_s\le B_s\\
                  s\in\mathcal B}}
 F_\eta(X,(x_s,y_s)_{s\in\mathcal B}).$$ An empty product of choices contributes one term.

</div>

Every box-valid full vector of total $T$ has strictly positive weight, regardless of its margin deviations or slot occupancies. Thus $f_\eta(X)>0$ for every box-valid $X$ of the indicated total. On the transversal and defect patterns, <a id="eq:soft-limit"></a>$$\label{eq:soft-limit}
 \lim_{\eta\downarrow0}f_\eta(X)=f(X).$$ Indeed, a term has $D=0$ exactly when the full row and column margins are $\bar r,\bar c$ and the small row and column totals do not exceed $r,c$. When the large slots are balanced, these conditions specify precisely the nonnegative residuals and padded completions defining $f$. On a line without a large slot, the full-margin equality also makes the residual zero. The capacity $C$ excludes no such completion, by its choice in <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a>. Each zero-penalty term tends to one, all other terms tend to zero, and the sum is finite.

<a id="eq:two-removal-total"></a>
We use the following convention throughout.
